I found the exact code in the compiler.py file. Now I can see the issue. At lines 1433-1434 and 1449:\n\n```python\n# Line 1433-1434\nif (\n    self.select[select_index][0].target.model == parent_model\n    or self.select[select_index][0].target.model in all_parents\n)\n\n# Line 1449\nif self.select[select_index][0].target.model == concrete_model:\n```\n\nThe issue is that not all expressions in `self.select` have a `.target` attribute. Only `Col` (column expressions) and similar field-based expressions have `.target`. Annotation expressions like `Concat()` don't have this attribute.\n\nLooking at line 214-217 in that nls_search result, I can see someone already using `hasattr(expr, \"target\")` to The `select_fields` list contained only indices of model columns.\n- **After regression** (Django 5.2, commit 65ad4ade74): The ordering changed to respect user-specified order via `values(*selected)`. Annotations can now appear BEFORE model field columns in `self.select`.\n- **The bug**: When iterating through `klass_info[\"select_fields\"]` indices, if an index points to an annotation expression (like `Concat()`), that expression lacks a `.target` attribute. Attempting to access `.target.model` raises `AttributeError: 'Concat' object has no attribute 'target'`.\n\n**Why `.target` Attribute Matters:**\n- `Col` and other field-based expressions have `.target` pointing to a `Field` object\n- Annotation expressions like `Concat()`, `F()`, `Value()`, `Case()` etc. are `Expression` subclasses without `.target`\n- The code needs `.target.model` to determine which database table a column belongs to for SELECT FOR UPDATE OF clause\n\n## Proposed Fix\n\nThe fix is in the `get_select()` method, not in `get_select_for_update_of_arguments()`. The solution ensures `select_fields` only contains indices of actual model field columns, never annotation expressions.\n\n**Root Cause of Bad Data**: In the `else` branch (lines 284-297) handling `self.query.selected`, the original code appended ALL indices to `select_fields`, including annotation references:\n\n```python\n# BEFORE FIX - BUG:\nfor alias, expression in self.query.selected.items():\n    if isinstance(expression, str):\n        # Annotation reference - NO INDEX ADDED (good)\n        expression = self.query.annotations[expression]\n    elif isinstance(expression, int):\n        # Column reference - INDEX ADDED (good)\n        expression = cols[expression]\n    selected.append((alias, expression))\n    # NO select_fields UPDATE - indices not tracked\n```\n\n**The Fix**:\n\n```diff\n--- a/django/db/models/sql/compiler.py\n+++ b/django/db/models/sql/compiler.py\n@@ -261,24 +261,28 @@ class SQLCompiler:\n         cols = self.get_default_columns(select_mask)\n     else:\n         # self.query.select is a special case. These columns never go to\n         # any model.\n         cols = self.query.select\n-    if cols:\n-        klass_info = {\n-            \"model\": self.query.model,\n-            \"select_fields\": list(\n-                range(\n-                    len(self.query.extra_select),\n-                    len(self.query.extra_select) + len(cols),\n-                )\n-            ),\n-        }\n     selected = []\n+    select_fields = None\n     if self.query.selected is None:\n         selected = [\n             *(...),\n             *((None, col) for col in cols),\n             *self.query.annotation_select.items(),\n         ]\n+        select_fields = list(\n+            range(\n+                len(self.query.extra_select),\n+                len(self.query.extra_select) + len(cols),\n+            )\n+        )\n     else:\n-        for alias, expression in self.query.selected.items():\n+        select_fields = []\n+        for index, (alias, expression) in enumerate(self.query.selected.items()):\n             # Reference to an annotation.\n             if isinstance(expression, str):\n                 expression = self.query.annotations[expression]\n             # Reference to a column.\n             elif isinstance(expression, int):\n+                select_fields.append(index)  # <-- CRITICAL FIX\n                 expression = cols[expression]\n             # ColPairs cannot be aliased.\n             if isinstance(expression, ColPairs):\n                 alias = None\n             selected.append((alias, expression))\n+    if select_fields:\n+        klass_info = {\"model\": self.query.model, \"select_fields\": select_fields}\n```\n\n**Key Change:**\n- Moved `klass_info` creation from unconditional (before) to conditional (only when `select_fields` exists)\n- Track indices in a local `select_fields` list during the loop\n- **CRITICAL**: Only append indices when `isinstance(expression, int)` (actual column), NOT when `isinstance(expression, str)` (annotation)\n- Ensures `select_fields` contains ONLY model field indices, never annotation indices\n\n## Analysis\n\n### Why the Bug Happened\n\nThe regression was introduced in commit 65ad4ade74 (2023-03-28) which refactored values selection to respect user-specified ordering. This commit added support for `Query.selected: dict[str, Any]` to track the global order of columns as specified by `values(*selected)`.\n\nBefore this change, the order was always: extra fields + model fields + annotations (in insertion order). This meant model field columns were always at predictable positions.\n\nAfter this change, users could specify any order, mixing annotations and fields:\n```python\n# New capability: annotations can come first\n.values_list(Concat(...), \"id\", \"name\")  # annotation before columns\n```\n\nHowever, the `get_select_for_update_of_arguments()` method retained its old assumption that model field columns always come first. It iterates through `select_fields` indices and directly accesses `.target.model` without verifying the expression has that attribute.\n\n### Why the Fix Works\n\nThe fix ensures that `select_fields` only contains indices of actual model field columns by:\n\n1. Initializing `select_fields` as an empty list in the `else` branch\n2. Only appending `index` when `isinstance(expression, int)` (meaning it's a reference to a column from `cols`)\n3. Never appending when `isinstance(expression, str)` (meaning it's an annotation reference)\n\nThis way, when `get_select_for_update_of_arguments()` iterates through `select_fields` and accesses `self.select[select_index][0]`, it will ALWAYS get a model field expression (which has `.target`), never an annotation.\n\n### Validation\n\nThe fix is validated by test case `test_for_update_of_values_list()`:\n```python\ndef test_for_update_of_values_list(self):\n    queries = Person.objects.select_for_update(\n        of=(\"self\",),\n    ).values_list(Concat(Value(\"Dr. \"), F(\"name\")), \"born\")\n    with transaction.atomic():\n        values = queries.get(pk=self.person.pk)\n    self.assertSequenceEqual(values, (\"Dr. Reinhardt\", self.city1.pk))\n```\n\nThis test combines:\n- `select_for_update(of=(\"self\",))` — requires model field column references\n- `values_list(Concat(...), \"born\")` — annotation appears BEFORE field column\n- Expected result: No AttributeError, query executes successfully\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":6,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2052,"cache_read_input_tokens":67653,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":2052},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a773b80f-6df9-4cc1-9187-b229de42ec02","uuid":"a2cc930e-2176-4dba-b3e3-eb1565b8ef79"}

This meant model field columns were always at predictable positions.\n\nAfter this change, users could specify any order, mixing annotations and fields:\n```python\n# New capability: annotations can come first\n.values_list(Concat(...), \"id\", \"name\")  # annotation before columns\n```\n\nHowever, the `get_select_for_update_of_arguments()` method retained its old assumption that model field columns always come first. It iterates through `select_fields` indices and directly accesses `.target.model` without verifying the expression has that attribute.\n\n### Why the Fix Works\n\nThe fix ensures that `select_fields` only contains indices of actual model field columns by:\n\n1. Initializing `select_fields` as an empty list in the `else` branch\n2. Only appending `index` when `isinstance(expression, int)` (meaning it's a reference to a column from `cols`)\n3. Never appending when `isinstance(expression, str)` (meaning it's an annotation reference)\n\nThis way, when `get_select_for_update_of_arguments()` iterates through `select_fields` and accesses `self.select[select_index][0]`, it will ALWAYS get a model field expression (which has `.target`), never an annotation.\n\n### Validation\n\nThe fix is validated by test case `test_for_update_of_values_list()`:\n```python\ndef test_for_update_of_values_list(self):\n    queries = Person.objects.select_for_update(\n        of=(\"self\",),\n    ).values_list(Concat(Value(\"Dr. \"), F(\"name\")), \"born\")\n    with transaction.atomic():\n        values = queries.get(pk=self.person.pk)\n    self.assertSequenceEqual(values, (\"Dr. Reinhardt\", self.city1.pk))\n```\n\nThis test combines:\n- `select_for_update(of=(\"self\",))` — requires model field column references\n- `values_list(Concat(...), \"born\")` — annotation appears BEFORE field column\n- Expected result: No AttributeError, query executes successfully\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
